[Novak Djokovic](16100029.md) (left) and [Jannik Sinner](60061043.md) (right) are the only singles players to complete the [Career Golden Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_Masters_Series_singles_records_and_statistics#Career_Golden_Masters), with Djokovic doing so twice. Djokovic also won a record 40 ATP Masters 1000 titles in singles. Sinner is the fastest player to complete the Golden Masters among all players.





The [Bryan brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bryan_brothers) (left) and [Daniel Nestor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Nestor) (right) are the only doubles players to complete the [Career Golden Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_Masters_Series_doubles_records_and_statistics#Career_Golden_Masters). The Bryan brothers also won a record 39 ATP Masters 1000 titles in doubles. Nestor is the first player to complete the Golden Masters among all players.

The **ATP Masters 1000** tournaments, also known as Masters 1000 or ATP 1000 events, are an annual series of nine tennis tournaments featuring the top-ranked players on the [ATP Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Tour) since its inception in 1990.[^cite_note-1] The ATP 1000 events sit below the [Grand Slam](197638.md#Tournaments) tournaments and the [year-end championships](1209231.md) in prestige on the annual ATP Tour calendar; these tournaments plus the quadrennial [Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_at_the_Summer_Olympics) are collectively known as the '[Big Titles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ATP_Big_Titles_singles_champions)'.[^cite_note-2]

In singles, [Novak Djokovic](16100029.md) holds the record for the [most ATP 1000 singles titles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Masters_1000_singles_records_and_statistics#Title_leaders) with 40.[^cite_note-3] By completing the career set of all nine current ATP 1000 series singles titles in [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Western_&_Southern_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Djokovic became the first player to achieve the [Career Golden Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Masters_1000_singles_records_and_statistics#Career_Golden_Masters) in singles.[^cite_note-GM-4] In 2026, [Jannik Sinner](60061043.md) became the second, and youngest, player to complete the Career Golden Masters at the age of 24.[^cite_note-5]

In doubles, the [Bryan brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bryan_brothers) ([Bob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Bryan) and [Mike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Bryan)) won a record [39 doubles titles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Masters_1000_doubles_records_and_statistics) as a team. [Daniel Nestor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Nestor) and the Bryan brothers are the only doubles players to achieve the [Career Golden Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Masters_1000_doubles_records_and_statistics#Career_Golden_Masters).[^cite_note-Masters_Sweep-6] All three doubles players also won titles at 10 different tournaments.&#x20;

Out of the five male players who achieved the **Career Golden Masters**, Daniel Nestor is the first player to achieve the feat in 2011[^cite_note-7] and Sinner is the fastest player to complete the same feat in a record span of 33 months.[^cite_note-8] In [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Western_&_Southern_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Djokovic has become the only player to complete the Career Golden Masters twice.[^cite_note-9]

## History

The ATP Championship Series (Single Week) was introduced in 1990 with the inception of the ATP Tour by bringing together the nine most prestigious tournaments of the [Grand Prix Super Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Prix_Super_Series) of the preceding [ITF Grand Prix Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ITF_Grand_Prix_Circuit). Results in ATP 1000 events earn players more ranking points than regular tournaments but less than Grand Slam events or the year-end ATP Finals.

Up until 2007, most ATP Masters 1000 finals were contested as best-of-five-set matches, but from 2008 all events were decided in best-of-three-set matches. As part of a shake-up of the tennis circuit in 2009, the Masters Series became the ATP Masters 1000,[^cite_note-10] with the addition of the number 1000 referring to the number of ranking points earned by the winner of each tournament. Contrary to earlier plans, the number of tournaments was not reduced from nine to eight and the [Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monte-Carlo_Masters) remained part of the series although, unlike the other events, it does not have a mandatory player commitment. The [Hamburg Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamburg_European_Open) event was downgraded to an [ATP Tour 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Tour_500) event. The [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrid_Open_\(tennis\)) moved to May and onto clay courts. A new tournament in [Shanghai](23203399.md) replaced the Hamburg Masters and took over Madrid's former October indoor slot.

In 2011, six of the nine 1000 level tournaments were combined ATP and [WTA](57768.md) events. In 2025, all tournaments introduced the [Video Review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instant_replay) system, starting with the [Indian Wells Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_BNP_Paribas_Open).[^cite_note-11]

### Series name

- 1990–1995: ATP Championship Series, Single Week

- 1996–1999: ATP Super 9

- 2000–2003: Tennis Masters Series

- 2004–2008: ATP Masters Series

- 2009–2018: ATP World Tour Masters 1000

- 2019–present: ATP Masters 1000

## Tournaments

Currently, the following nine tournaments are part of the ATP Masters 1000: [Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Open_\(tennis\)) (alternating yearly between [Montreal](7954681.md) and [Toronto](64646.md)), [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Open_\(tennis\)) (held in [Rome](25458.md)), [Indian Wells Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Wells_Open), [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Open_\(tennis\)), [Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monte-Carlo_Masters), [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrid_Open_\(tennis\)), [Cincinnati Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cincinnati_Open), [Shanghai Masters](23203399.md) and [Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Masters).[^cite_note-16] Since [2009](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_ATP_World_Tour), five of the tournaments have been held on outdoor hard courts, three on clay and one on indoor hard court, whereas from [1990](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_ATP_Tour) until [2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_ATP_Tour) there were two indoor tournaments at the top-9 level. In 2009, the [Shanghai Masters](23203399.md) replaced the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrid_Open_\(tennis\)), which was until then held as an indoor event, in the eighth slot of the year with the Madrid Open switched to clay courts, replacing the [Hamburg Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamburg_Open) in the spring clay court season. The Shanghai Masters was designated as an outdoor event despite the facility having a retractable roof and having been used as the indoor venue for the ATP Finals from 2005 until 2008. Other than Hamburg, the tournaments defunct between 1990 and 2009 were Stockholm (1990–1994) and Stuttgart (1995–2001), which were held as indoor events in the eighth slot.

On October 23, 2025, the ATP announced that a new Masters 1000 tournament (the tenth on the calendar) will be held in [Saudi Arabia](349303.md), likely beginning in 2028, although the specific date remains to be determined. The placement of the event on the ATP calendar has also not been announced. The field will be 56 players and the event will be non-mandatory. This will be the first time the ATP Masters 1000 events number ten in one calendar year.[^cite_note-17]

[^cite_note-13]: &#x20;As the *ATP Masters Series* (2004–2008), winners were awarded 500 points and finalists were awarded 350 points.

[^cite_note-bye-15]: &#x20;Players with [byes](2086074.md#Bye) losing their first match receive first round points.<sup>[\[13\]](#cite_note-atp_points-14)</sup>

[^cite_note-france-18]: &#x20;The Monte-Carlo Masters, despite its name, is held in Roquebrune-Cap-Martin, France, not in [Monaco](19261.md).

[^cite_note-Madrid-19]: &#x20;Madrid Masters was played in the [Madrid Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrid_Arena) on an indoor hardcourt from 2002 to 2008.

[^cite_note-canada-20]: &#x20;The men's Canadian Open is held in Montreal in even-numbered years and Toronto in odd-numbered years, alternating with the women's event.

[^cite_note-moremasters-25]: &#x20;They additionally won the previously held [Madrid Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrid_Open_\(tennis\)) on indoors and the [Hamburg Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamburg_Open) on clay.
